# Multilingual Specialty-Coffee Brewing Terminology Research

**Research execution date:** 2026-08-06
**Source access-date range:** 2026-08-06 to 2026-08-06
**Research scope:** 22 locales × 12 required concepts = 264 locale–concept records.
**Native review:** No qualified human native-speaking specialty-coffee reviewer was available. Every record is marked `researched_not_native_reviewed`.

## Research header and completion manifest

- Required records accounted for: **264/264**.
- Records with a supported substantive classification: **224**.
- Records classified `INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`: **40**.
- Records not attempted or omitted: **0**.
- Records awaiting qualified native review: **264**.
- Retained source pages: **115**; text-status mix: original=101, translated=10, unclear=4.

### Exact regional markets examined

- **German (`de`):** Germany, Austria, and German-speaking Switzerland.
- **Spanish (`es`):** Spain, Mexico, Colombia, Argentina, Chile, and Peru. Coverage is incomplete for Latin America as a whole.
- **French (`fr`):** France primary; Belgium, French-speaking Switzerland, and Quebec screened.
- **Portuguese (`pt`):** Portugal and Brazil researched separately.
- **Simplified Chinese (`zh-CN`):** Mainland China only. Taiwan/Hong Kong Traditional Chinese evidence was excluded from `zh-CN` conclusions.
- Other locale coverage is stated in each locale section; no unsupported cross-language transfer was used.

### Researcher and tool limitations

- No qualified human native-speaking specialty-coffee reviewer was available; model multilingual ability was not counted as native review.
- Research was limited to web-accessible material. No interviews, private professional groups, offline books, paywalled training manuals, or in-person speech corpus were available.
- Video and podcast evidence was used only where reliable searchable text was available; inaccessible captions were not treated as evidence.
- Search-engine discovery is not a frequency corpus. Prevalence judgments rely on convergence and source diversity, not result counts.
- Publisher-level deduplication reduces obvious dependence but cannot prove that every editorial source was independently authored.
- Some markets have small native specialty-coffee web corpora and substantial translated retail content; those records were downgraded or marked insufficient.
- Source pages can change after the access date; excerpts and metadata represent what was retained on 2026-08-06.

## Cross-locale synthesis

**Evidence-supported pattern.** `coffee_grounds`, `filter_paper`, and most `valve` labels are usually localized. The most persistent English-bearing fields are `brewer_dripper`, `bloom`, `fines`, `drawdown`, named `swirl_spin` techniques, and the professional method label `immersion`. `slurry` is the major exception to both simple localization and simple English retention: most locales either paraphrase the coffee–water mixture or lack enough evidence for a conventional lexical item.

**Audience and context pattern.** Professionals and enthusiasts more readily use dripper, bloom/blooming, fines, drawdown, server, immersion, swirl, or Rao Spin. Beginner material more often uses a local device noun, an explicit “initial wetting/pre-infusion” phrase, a local action verb, and a generic jug/carafe. English nouns are frequently integrated into local articles, cases, compounds, suffixes, or verb derivations rather than remaining grammatically isolated.

**Regional pattern.** Portuguese has the strongest supported intra-language split in this corpus, especially Brazil versus Portugal for the dripper, bloom, grounds, drawdown, and receiving vessel. Quebec contributes `éclosion` as a bloom explanation, while France/Belgium/Romandy more often foreground `pré-infusion`. German-speaking markets show preference differences rather than a clean lexical border. The sampled Spanish markets converge on core localized basics, but the sample is not evidence for every Latin American market.

**Largest gaps.** Lithuanian, Latvian, Slovenian, Estonian, Greek, Croatian, and several advanced concepts in other small web corpora require targeted native review. Across languages, `slurry`, `drawdown`, and `fines` have the greatest risk of false precision; dry ground coffee versus spent grounds is the most consequential semantic distinction for ordinary UI copy.

**Product-design judgment.** A single permanent “show English brewing terms” preference is not supported by the evidence. The better default is localized copy with concept-specific English exposure: first occurrence or tooltip for established mixed terms, glossary and search aliases generally, persistent dual labels only where both forms are genuinely normal, and no guessed label for insufficient records.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Bulgaria; web-accessible Bulgarian specialty-coffee sources. Retained sources: 5. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=8, MIXED_STABLE=2, INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE=2. Confidence after QC: HIGH=1, MEDIUM=9, LOW=2.
This locale has a complete 12-record manifest but only partial evidentiary completion: insufficient records are present and explicitly blocked from confident lexical recommendation.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Adapt by concept: local basics; retain bloom/dripper aliases contextually; do not ship unresolved bed/slurry labels without review.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, fines. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

English loans appear with Bulgarian articles/endings in informal writing (e.g. дрипърът/дрипъра); Cyrillic local forms are preferable in beginner copy.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: `brewer_dripper`: дрипъра, дрипърът; `bloom`: блуминг, blooming фаза

### 4. Regional differences

No supported internal regional split; coverage outside major Bulgarian online specialty-coffee sources is limited.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

### 5. Concept-by-concept evidence

- **Classification / confidence:** `MIXED_STABLE` / `MEDIUM`.

- **Beginner usage:** дрипър or филтърен конус

#### `coffee_bed` — No stable term established

- **Classification / confidence:** `INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE` / `LOW`.
- **Professional usage:** No reliable Bulgarian specialty convention was established.
- **Beginner usage:** Explain descriptively as слой от смляно кафе until reviewed.
- **Qualification:** Searches found descriptive wording but not enough independent specialty evidence. Target-language searches covered native candidates, English forms embedded in local prose, localized paraphrases, specialist recipes, equipment pages, and available manuals. Explicit retained candidate labels/search targets: coffee bed. The retained corpus was too sparse, indirect, translation-heavy, or contradictory to establish convention. Resolution requires at least two independent native specialty-coffee evidence chains and targeted human review.
- No retained native-language evidence met the threshold. The record therefore remains explicitly uncertain; see the search-gap note in the structured record.

Recommended review scope: coffee_bed, slurry. Use a native-speaking specialty-coffee professional, educator, or demonstrably domain-qualified translator; test both written UI labels and spoken instruction phrasing.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Czechia. Retained sources: 7. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=4, MIXED_STABLE=3, AUDIENCE_DEPENDENT=4, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1. Confidence after QC: HIGH=8, MEDIUM=4.
All 12 concepts have a substantive classification, but none has qualified human native review.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Adapt by concept: local terms for basic objects and actions; contextual English for blooming, drawdown, fines, server and advanced technique names.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, fines, drawdown, swirl_spin, server_carafe, steeping_immersion. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom, drawdown, server_carafe; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

Loanwords decline in Czech (dripper/dripperu, server/serveru); blooming is commonly paired with kvetení or nakvetnutí.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: `brewer_dripper`: dripperu, dripperem, drippery; `bloom`: bloomingu, bloomingem; `server_carafe`: serveru, serverem

### 4. Regional differences

No material Czech regional split evidenced.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Denmark. Retained sources: 7. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=6, MIXED_STABLE=2, AUDIENCE_DEPENDENT=2, CONTEXT_DEPENDENT=1, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1. Confidence after QC: HIGH=6, MEDIUM=6.
All 12 concepts have a substantive classification, but none has qualified human native review.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Use Danish hardware/action terms; retain bloom and immersion as contextual/search aliases and for enthusiast material.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, fines, swirl_spin, steeping_immersion. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom, steeping_immersion; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

English nouns readily form Danish compounds or take Danish syntax (immersion-brygning, bloom-fase); local verbs remain more natural in instructions.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: `steeping_immersion`: immersion-brygning, immersion-metode

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, fines, slurry, drawdown, swirl_spin, server_carafe, steeping_immersion. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom, drawdown, steeping_immersion; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

English nouns commonly take German articles and compounds; capitalization follows noun status (der Bloom, die Fines; Kaffeebett, Durchlaufzeit).
Retained adapted or inflected forms: `brewer_dripper`: Drippers, Drippern; `bloom`: der Bloom, beim Blooming

### 4. Regional differences

No decisive country split for core labels; Swiss sources lean visibly toward Durchlaufzeit and German explanatory wording, while German/Austrian enthusiast material more readily retains Drawdown.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Greece; Cyprus was not adequately covered. Retained sources: 4. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=7, MIXED_STABLE=1, AUDIENCE_DEPENDENT=1, INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE=3. Confidence after QC: HIGH=2, MEDIUM=7, LOW=3.
This locale has a complete 12-record manifest but only partial evidentiary completion: insufficient records are present and explicitly blocked from confident lexical recommendation.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Use established Greek basics; show dripper and blooming contextually; block low-evidence fines, slurry and drawdown labels pending native review.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, swirl_spin. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

English loans are usually indeclinable in Greek text and take Greek articles; process explanations use προεκχύλιση/άνθιση.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: none sufficiently evidenced beyond the preferred forms.

### 6. Additional English-derived terms

Adjacent or adapted English-derived labels materially connected to the required concepts in this corpus:
- dripper (brewer_dripper; professional_only)
- blooming (bloom; professional_only)
- Rao spin (swirl_spin; professional_only)
- server (server_carafe; professional_only)
These are aliases or named techniques, not additional required concept records; their presence does not by itself make English dominant.

### 7. Unresolved questions

Priority unresolved or low-confidence records: fines (INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE, LOW); slurry (INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE, LOW); drawdown (INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE, LOW).
Needed evidence: one professional/authoritative native source, one independent practical native source, explicit semantic context, and a reviewer judgment on spoken naturalness and beginner comprehension.

### 8. Native-review status and recommendation

Status for all 12 records: `researched_not_native_reviewed`. No model fluency, anonymous comment, or inferred identity was counted as native review.
Recommended review scope: fines, slurry, drawdown. Use a native-speaking specialty-coffee professional, educator, or demonstrably domain-qualified translator; test both written UI labels and spoken instruction phrasing.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Spain, Mexico, Colombia, Argentina, Chile, and Peru; not all Latin American markets. Retained sources: 8. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=4, MIXED_STABLE=2, AUDIENCE_DEPENDENT=3, CONTEXT_DEPENDENT=2, INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE=1. Confidence after QC: HIGH=8, MEDIUM=3, LOW=1.
This locale has a complete 12-record manifest but only partial evidentiary completion: insufficient records are present and explicitly blocked from confident lexical recommendation.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Use local defaults (preinfusión, café molido, filtro de papel); retain dripper, bloom and Rao Spin contextually; keep regional vessel/device aliases searchable.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, fines, drawdown, swirl_spin, server_carafe. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

Dripper/dríper varies in spelling and gender; bloom is usually masculine in specialist speech; local verbs (girar, hacer círculos) are clearer than English action nouns.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: `brewer_dripper`: el dripper, el dríper

### 4. Regional differences

The sampled countries converge on café molido, preinfusión, filtro de papel and inmersión. Device and receiving-vessel labels vary by author more than by a proven country boundary. Coverage must not be generalized to all Latin America.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

These are aliases or named techniques, not additional required concept records; their presence does not by itself make English dominant.

### 7. Unresolved questions

Priority unresolved or low-confidence records: slurry (INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE, LOW).
Needed evidence: one professional/authoritative native source, one independent practical native source, explicit semantic context, and a reviewer judgment on spoken naturalness and beginner comprehension.

### 8. Native-review status and recommendation

Status for all 12 records: `researched_not_native_reviewed`. No model fluency, anonymous comment, or inferred identity was counted as native review.
Recommended review scope: slurry. Use a native-speaking specialty-coffee professional, educator, or demonstrably domain-qualified translator; test both written UI labels and spoken instruction phrasing.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Estonia; small web corpus, with several translated commerce pages. Retained sources: 4. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=3, CONTEXT_DEPENDENT=2, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1, INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE=6. Confidence after QC: HIGH=1, MEDIUM=2, LOW=9.
This locale has a complete 12-record manifest but only partial evidentiary completion: insufficient records are present and explicitly blocked from confident lexical recommendation.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Ship only well-attested basics; expose English only as search aliases; withhold the unresolved bed, bloom, fines, drawdown, immersion and valve records pending review.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for bloom. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for none; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

Compounding is productive (paberfilter, klaaskann); English coffee jargon is often left uninflected or embedded in Estonian case syntax, but the sparse corpus prevents strong generalization.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: none sufficiently evidenced beyond the preferred forms.

### 4. Regional differences

No regional split assessed.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Finland. Retained sources: 4. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=6, MIXED_STABLE=1, CONTEXT_DEPENDENT=2, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1, INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE=2. Confidence after QC: HIGH=6, MEDIUM=4, LOW=2.
This locale has a complete 12-record manifest but only partial evidentiary completion: insufficient records are present and explicitly blocked from confident lexical recommendation.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Use Finnish hardware and action terms; show blooming/immersion contextually in enthusiast guidance; no shipped lexical label for slurry.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, steeping_immersion. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom, steeping_immersion; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

Compounds are normal (suodatinsuppilo, suodatinpaperi); English blooming often appears quoted, while Finnish verbs carry instructional grammar.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: `bloom`: "blooming"-vaihe, blooming-vaiheen

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: France primary; Belgium, Romandy, and Quebec screened. Retained sources: 7. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=6, MIXED_STABLE=1, CONTEXT_DEPENDENT=4, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1. Confidence after QC: HIGH=8, MEDIUM=4.
All 12 concepts have a substantive classification, but none has qualified human native review.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Use French defaults; show bloom/dripper/server only contextually or as search aliases; allow Quebec éclosion as a regional explanatory alias.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, fines, swirl_spin, server_carafe, steeping_immersion. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom, steeping_immersion; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

Porte-filtre is hyphenated; café moulu and marc de café must remain distinct; English bloom/dripper take French articles.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: none sufficiently evidenced beyond the preferred forms.

### 4. Regional differences

Quebec evidence includes éclosion for bloom explanation; France/Belgium/Romandy more often use pré-infusion. Device labels alternate between dripper and porte-filtre across all screened regions rather than forming a clean border.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

### 6. Additional English-derived terms

Adjacent or adapted English-derived labels materially connected to the required concepts in this corpus:
- dripper (brewer_dripper; professional_only)
- cône V60 (brewer_dripper; accepted)
- bloom (bloom; professional_only)
- fines (fines; professional_only)
- slurry (slurry; professional_only)
- drawdown (drawdown; professional_only)
- Rao Spin (swirl_spin; professional_only)
These are aliases or named techniques, not additional required concept records; their presence does not by itself make English dominant.

### 7. Unresolved questions

No record is `LOW` or `INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`; regional and spoken-language validation still remains open because no human reviewer participated.

### 8. Native-review status and recommendation

Status for all 12 records: `researched_not_native_reviewed`. No model fluency, anonymous comment, or inferred identity was counted as native review.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Use Croatian basics and contextual dripper/blooming; withhold fines, slurry and drawdown until native specialty review.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, swirl_spin, steeping_immersion. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

Dripper is adapted through Croatian case endings (dripperu); blooming is often combined with cvjetanje/predinfuzija.

English terms take Hungarian suffixes (bloomnak, bloomingnak); compounds such as kávéágy and filterpapír are natural.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Italy. Retained sources: 5. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=5, ENGLISH_DOMINANT=1, MIXED_STABLE=2, AUDIENCE_DEPENDENT=3, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1. Confidence after QC: HIGH=8, MEDIUM=4.
All 12 concepts have a substantive classification, but none has qualified human native review.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Use dripper as the established specialist device label, Italian terms for basic concepts, and contextual dual labels for blooming, server and swirl techniques.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, fines, swirl_spin, server_carafe, steeping_immersion. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom, server_carafe, steeping_immersion; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

Dripper and server are generally indeclinable masculine loans; blooming coexists with pre-infusione/fioritura; caffè macinato differs from fondi.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: `brewer_dripper`: il dripper, dei dripper

### 4. Regional differences

No material regional split evidenced.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Lithuania; small native specialty corpus and substantial translated retail material. Retained sources: 3. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=3, CONTEXT_DEPENDENT=1, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1, INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE=7. Confidence after QC: HIGH=2, MEDIUM=1, LOW=9.
This locale has a complete 12-record manifest but only partial evidentiary completion: insufficient records are present and explicitly blocked from confident lexical recommendation.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior F: Do not ship unresolved terminology until additional research or native review is completed.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Do not ship the unresolved advanced terminology set without native specialty-coffee review; well-attested basics may ship behind concept-specific fallbacks.

**Product-design judgment:** This conservative behavior sacrifices immediate terminology coverage to avoid shipping machine-like or weakly evidenced labels. It improves naturalness and accessibility for supported basics, but requires explanatory fallbacks and higher maintenance until review closes the gaps.

**English placement:** Do not expose guessed labels for coffee_bed, bloom, fines, drawdown, swirl_spin, steeping_immersion, valve. For supported basics, use the local term; keep English in search aliases and an expandable glossary, not beside every instruction.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

Native inflection applies to localized terms; imported dripper labels occur inconsistently. Avoid treating automatically translated product text as convention.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: none sufficiently evidenced beyond the preferred forms.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior F: Do not ship unresolved terminology until additional research or native review is completed.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Ship only ziedēšana, malta kafija, karafe and papīra filtrs after targeted review; withhold unresolved advanced labels.

**Product-design judgment:** This conservative behavior sacrifices immediate terminology coverage to avoid shipping machine-like or weakly evidenced labels. It improves naturalness and accessibility for supported basics, but requires explanatory fallbacks and higher maintenance until review closes the gaps.

**English placement:** Do not expose guessed labels for coffee_bed, fines, drawdown, swirl_spin, steeping_immersion, valve. For supported basics, use the local term; keep English in search aliases and an expandable glossary, not beside every instruction.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Netherlands primary; Belgium/Flanders was not systematically covered. Retained sources: 6. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=5, ENGLISH_DOMINANT=1, MIXED_STABLE=1, AUDIENCE_DEPENDENT=3, CONTEXT_DEPENDENT=1, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1. Confidence after QC: HIGH=9, MEDIUM=3.
All 12 concepts have a substantive classification, but none has qualified human native review.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Use Dutch basics; allow bloom/bloomen, dripper, immersion and swirl as concept-specific specialist labels and aliases.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, fines, swirl_spin, server_carafe, steeping_immersion. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom, steeping_immersion; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

Bloom produces the Dutch verb bloomen; compounds such as koffiebed and doorlooptijd are standard-looking and well attested.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: `brewer_dripper`: dripper, drippers; `bloom`: de bloom, bloomen, gebloomd

### 4. Regional differences

Flanders coverage is incomplete; no region split claimed.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

Status for all 12 records: `researched_not_native_reviewed`. No model fluency, anonymous comment, or inferred identity was counted as native review.
Recommended review scope: brewer_dripper, fines, swirl_spin, server_carafe. Use a native-speaking specialty-coffee professional, educator, or demonstrably domain-qualified translator; test both written UI labels and spoken instruction phrasing.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Poland. Retained sources: 6. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=4, ENGLISH_DOMINANT=1, MIXED_STABLE=3, AUDIENCE_DEPENDENT=3, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1. Confidence after QC: HIGH=8, MEDIUM=4.
All 12 concepts have a substantive classification, but none has qualified human native review.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Use local basics; retain established specialist terms bloom, drawdown, swirl and immersion contextually, with Polish explanations for beginners.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, fines, drawdown, swirl_spin, server_carafe, steeping_immersion. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom, drawdown, swirl_spin, server_carafe, steeping_immersion; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

English loans take Polish case endings in speech/writing (drippera); specialist glossaries stabilize English nouns while instructions use Polish verbs.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: `brewer_dripper`: drippera, dripperem, drippery

### 4. Regional differences

No material regional split evidenced.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

Adjacent or adapted English-derived labels materially connected to the required concepts in this corpus:
- dripper (brewer_dripper; professional_only)
- blooming (bloom; professional_only)
- fines (fines; professional_only)
- slurry (slurry; professional_only)
- drawdown (drawdown; professional_only)
- Rao Spin (swirl_spin; professional_only)
These are aliases or named techniques, not additional required concept records; their presence does not by itself make English dominant.

### 7. Unresolved questions

No record is `LOW` or `INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`; regional and spoken-language validation still remains open because no human reviewer participated.

### 8. Native-review status and recommendation

Status for all 12 records: `researched_not_native_reviewed`. No model fluency, anonymous comment, or inferred identity was counted as native review.
Recommended review scope: brewer_dripper, fines, server_carafe. Use a native-speaking specialty-coffee professional, educator, or demonstrably domain-qualified translator; test both written UI labels and spoken instruction phrasing.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Portugal and Brazil researched separately. Retained sources: 7. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=6, REGION_DEPENDENT=5, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1. Confidence after QC: HIGH=7, MEDIUM=5.
All 12 concepts have a substantive classification, but none has qualified human native review.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Use region-aware labels: Brazilian coador/porta-filtro and pré-infusão; Portuguese V60/dripper/brewer specialist aliases. Preserve local jarra/servidor variants.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, fines, drawdown, swirl_spin, server_carafe. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for none; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

English loans are generally indeclinable; Brazilian prose favors coador/porta-filtro and pó de café, while both markets use Portuguese action grammar.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: none sufficiently evidenced beyond the preferred forms.

### 4. Regional differences

A supported Portugal–Brazil distinction exists for device and some specialist labels. Portugal specialist sources retain bloom/drawdown more readily; Brazil favors coador/porta-filtro and pré-infusão.
- `brewer_dripper` — Brazil: **coador / porta-filtro** (Beginner and editorial usage.)
- `brewer_dripper` — Portugal: **V60 / dripper / brewer** (Specialist sources retain more English equipment language.)
- `bloom` — Brazil: **pré-infusão (bloom)** (Localized term foregrounded.)
- `bloom` — Portugal: **bloom / pré-infusão** (English specialist term more visible.)
- `coffee_grounds` — Brazil: **pó de café / café moído** (Both common.)
- `coffee_grounds` — Portugal: **café moído** (Preferred neutral form.)
- `drawdown` — Portugal: **drawdown / tempo total** (English label directly attested in specialist product/recipe copy.)
- `drawdown` — Brazil: **tempo de escoamento / gotejamento** (Localized description favored.)
- `server_carafe` — Brazil: **jarra** (Beginner/editorial default.)
- `server_carafe` — Portugal: **servidor / jarro** (Specialist/product alternatives.)

### 6. Additional English-derived terms

Adjacent or adapted English-derived labels materially connected to the required concepts in this corpus:
- dripper (brewer_dripper; professional_only)
- brewer (brewer_dripper; professional_only)
- bloom (bloom; professional_only)
- fines (fines; professional_only)
- slurry (slurry; professional_only)
- drawdown (drawdown; regional)
- Rao Spin (swirl_spin; professional_only)
These are aliases or named techniques, not additional required concept records; their presence does not by itself make English dominant.

### 7. Unresolved questions

No record is `LOW` or `INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`; regional and spoken-language validation still remains open because no human reviewer participated.

### 8. Native-review status and recommendation

Status for all 12 records: `researched_not_native_reviewed`. No model fluency, anonymous comment, or inferred identity was counted as native review.
Recommended review scope: brewer_dripper, bloom, coffee_grounds, drawdown, server_carafe. Use a native-speaking specialty-coffee professional, educator, or demonstrably domain-qualified translator; test both written UI labels and spoken instruction phrasing.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Romania. Retained sources: 5. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=6, ENGLISH_DOMINANT=2, MIXED_STABLE=3, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1. Confidence after QC: HIGH=9, MEDIUM=3.
All 12 concepts have a substantive classification, but none has qualified human native review.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Use dripper/brewer and bloom where established, but pair them with Romanian explanations on first occurrence; use Romanian defaults for the remaining basics.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, swirl_spin, steeping_immersion. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom, swirl_spin; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

Dripper and brewer are indeclinable or take Romanian articles variably; bloom is often explained through înflorire; pat de cafea and strat de cafea coexist.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: `brewer_dripper`: dripperul, brewerului; `bloom`: bloom-ul, al doilea bloom

### 4. Regional differences

No material regional split evidenced.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

### 7. Unresolved questions

No record is `LOW` or `INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`; regional and spoken-language validation still remains open because no human reviewer participated.

### 8. Native-review status and recommendation

Status for all 12 records: `researched_not_native_reviewed`. No model fluency, anonymous comment, or inferred identity was counted as native review.
Recommended review scope: all terms in a final consistency pass. Use a native-speaking specialty-coffee professional, educator, or demonstrably domain-qualified translator; test both written UI labels and spoken instruction phrasing.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Slovakia. Retained sources: 4. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=5, ENGLISH_DOMINANT=1, MIXED_STABLE=4, AUDIENCE_DEPENDENT=1, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1. Confidence after QC: HIGH=6, MEDIUM=6.
All 12 concepts have a substantive classification, but none has qualified human native review.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Use dripper, blooming and server for specialist recognition with localized explanations; local terms for bed, drainage, valve and paper filter.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, fines, swirl_spin, server_carafe, steeping_immersion. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for brewer_dripper, bloom, fines, swirl_spin, server_carafe, steeping_immersion; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

English loans take Slovak endings (drippera, serveri); blooming coexists with predinfúzia and kvitnutie; imerzia is a localized internationalism.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: `brewer_dripper`: drippera, dripperom, drippery; `bloom`: bloomingu, blooming fáza; `server_carafe`: serveri, servera

### 4. Regional differences

No material regional split evidenced.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Slovenia; limited specialist corpus. Retained sources: 3. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=4, ENGLISH_DOMINANT=1, MIXED_STABLE=1, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1, INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE=5. Confidence after QC: HIGH=3, MEDIUM=2, LOW=7.
This locale has a complete 12-record manifest but only partial evidentiary completion: insufficient records are present and explicitly blocked from confident lexical recommendation.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior F: Do not ship unresolved terminology until additional research or native review is completed.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Ship only well-attested basics and blooming/kavni cvet after native review; withhold unresolved bed, fines, drawdown, immersion and valve labels.

**Product-design judgment:** This conservative behavior sacrifices immediate terminology coverage to avoid shipping machine-like or weakly evidenced labels. It improves naturalness and accessibility for supported basics, but requires explanatory fallbacks and higher maintenance until review closes the gaps.

**English placement:** Do not expose guessed labels for coffee_bed, fines, drawdown, steeping_immersion, valve. For supported basics, use the local term; keep English in search aliases and an expandable glossary, not beside every instruction.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

Dripper takes Slovene case endings (dripperju); blooming is paired with kavni cvet; translated retail pages must not define convention.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: `brewer_dripper`: dripperju, dripperja

No regional split assessed.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Sweden. Retained sources: 6. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=6, MIXED_STABLE=2, AUDIENCE_DEPENDENT=1, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1, INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE=2. Confidence after QC: HIGH=6, MEDIUM=3, LOW=3.
This locale has a complete 12-record manifest but only partial evidentiary completion: insufficient records are present and explicitly blocked from confident lexical recommendation.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior E: Adapt behavior by concept rather than applying one locale-wide rule.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Use Swedish hardware and action terms; retain bloom, immersion and swirl as contextual aliases; withhold bed and fines labels pending review.

**Product-design judgment:** Concept-level behavior best balances naturalness and beginner comprehension against professional recognition and searchability. It avoids permanent dual-label clutter, preserves English where authentic, and costs more to maintain than one locale-wide switch because display policy must be stored per concept.

**English placement:** Use English on first occurrence or via tap/tooltip for brewer_dripper, bloom, swirl_spin, steeping_immersion. Persistent dual labels are justified only selectively for bloom, steeping_immersion; all accepted English forms may remain search aliases and glossary entries.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

Compounds dominate (filterhållare, pappersfilter); immersion is productive in compounds, while Swedish verbs are clearer for actions.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: none sufficiently evidenced beyond the preferred forms.

### 4. Regional differences

No material regional split evidenced.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

### 1. Locale summary

Coverage: Mainland China only; Traditional Chinese sources from Taiwan/Hong Kong were excluded from conclusions. Retained sources: 6. Classification profile: LOCALIZED_DOMINANT=10, CONTEXT_DEPENDENT=1, NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM=1. Confidence after QC: HIGH=5, MEDIUM=7.
All 12 concepts have a substantive classification, but none has qualified human native review.

### 2. Application behavior recommendation

**Primary behavior B: Show the localized term, with English available contextually.**

**Evidence-supported terminology finding:** Show localized terminology by default; expose English only in glossary/search and for named techniques such as Rao Spin.

**Product-design judgment:** Localized defaults maximize naturalness, beginner comprehension, accessibility, and consistency. Contextual English preserves searchability and transfer to international resources without the visual load of permanent dual labels; maintenance remains concept-level but modest.

**English placement:** English should not be persistent. Put established English aliases in search and the glossary; use a first-occurrence tooltip only for advanced material and named techniques such as Rao Spin.

### 3. Important grammatical and inflection notes

Compact noun compounds are conventional (滤杯, 粉床, 分享壶); action phrases specify the object rotated. English names may follow in parentheses in advanced content.
Retained adapted or inflected forms: none sufficiently evidenced beyond the preferred forms.

### 4. Regional differences

Mainland-only conclusion. Taiwan and Hong Kong terminology was not treated as zh-CN evidence.
- No concept-level regional variant met the evidence threshold.

### 6. Additional English-derived terms

Adjacent or adapted English-derived labels materially connected to the required concepts in this corpus:
- dripper (brewer_dripper; professional_only)
- bloom (bloom; professional_only)
- slurry (slurry; professional_only)
- Rao Spin (swirl_spin; professional_only)
These are aliases or named techniques, not additional required concept records; their presence does not by itself make English dominant.

### 7. Unresolved questions

No record is `LOW` or `INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE`; regional and spoken-language validation still remains open because no human reviewer participated.

### 8. Native-review status and recommendation

Status for all 12 records: `researched_not_native_reviewed`. No model fluency, anonymous comment, or inferred identity was counted as native review.
Recommended review scope: drawdown. Use a native-speaking specialty-coffee professional, educator, or demonstrably domain-qualified translator; test both written UI labels and spoken instruction phrasing.

Total: **40** records. These should use a descriptive fallback, remain hidden, or be blocked until targeted evidence and native review are obtained.

### 6. Regional exceptions preventing a single language-wide term

- **Portuguese:** Brazil and Portugal require region-aware labels for the dripper, bloom, dry grounds, drawdown, and receiving vessel.
- **French:** Quebec `éclosion` is a regional bloom explanation; `pré-infusion` remains the safer cross-regional default.
- **German:** Germany/Austria show greater visibility of English advanced terms, while Swiss material more often foregrounds explanatory German; this is a preference gradient, not a fully separate lexicon.
- **Spanish:** the six sampled markets do not justify a country split for the core basics, but this does not support generalization to all Latin America. Keep regional aliases available and collect market telemetry/review before expanding coverage.
- **Chinese:** Mainland Simplified Chinese conclusions must not be reused as Taiwan/Hong Kong Traditional Chinese terminology.

### 7. Recommendation for the “show English brewing terms” preference

**Revise and effectively replace the global preference.** A single toggle forces the same treatment onto concepts whose evidence differs sharply. Keep an optional advanced-language preference only as a secondary override; the default rendering should be determined by locale × concept × audience/context metadata.

### 8. Recommended English-reference behavior

- **First occurrence:** use for stable mixed terms such as bloom/dripper/drawdown where the local corpus genuinely pairs the forms.
- **Tap, expansion, or tooltip:** default mechanism for professional aliases and named techniques.
- **Glossary:** include every accepted English alias, semantic boundary, and dry-versus-spent warning.
- **Search alias:** index English, localized, adapted, inflected, and regional forms without forcing them into display copy.
- **Persistent dual labels:** reserve for a small set of `MIXED_STABLE` records where both forms are normal for overlapping audiences; do not apply globally.
- **Concept-specific suppression:** do not expose English merely because no local term was established; `slurry` often needs a descriptive sentence rather than an English label.

### 9. Terminology data-model changes required by the findings

| Field | Why the corpus requires it |
|---|---|
| `locale` + optional `region` | Portuguese and selected French/German evidence cannot always be represented by one language-wide label. |
| `concept_id` | Prevents accidental collapse of device/person, dry/spent grounds, vessel types, process/action, and method/duration. |
| `preferred_display_term` and `normalized_search_form` | Natural typography/inflection must be preserved while search needs NFC, lowercase, and lemma-like aliases. |
| `english_aliases` + `alias_status` | English may be preferred, professional-only, regional, or merely searchable; a boolean cannot express this. |
| `inflected_or_adapted_forms` | Czech, Croatian, Hungarian, Bulgarian, Danish, German and other languages embed loans in local grammar. |
| `audience` / `display_policy` | Dripper, bloom, fines, server, drawdown and immersion systematically differ between professional and beginner material. |
| `method_scope` / `usage_context` | The same word may be safe in pour-over but misleading in immersion, espresso, batch brewing, or for a named technique. |
| `regional_variants` | Portugal/Brazil and smaller regional exceptions require explicit market scoping. |
| `avoid_terms` + reason | False friends and semantic traps—especially dry grounds versus spent grounds—must be blocked, not merely deprioritized. |
| `confidence`, `classification`, `evidence_provenance`, `research_date`, `review_status` | The corpus contains mixed, context-dependent, and insufficient records; shipping decisions need traceability and staleness/review controls. |
| `fallback_behavior` | `INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE` and `NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM` require explanatory copy, English fallback, or feature blocking rather than a fabricated translation. |
| `first_occurrence_gloss` / `glossary_entry` | Contextual English gives transfer value without persistent visual load. |
Do not add complexity where the observed corpus does not require it; the fields above each correspond to a documented distinction or failure mode in the matrix.

### 10. Evidence, product judgment, and unresolved uncertainty

**Evidence-supported findings:** the locale–concept terms, classifications, alternatives, grammatical forms, short native excerpts, source metadata, confidence after QC, and documented regional differences in the 264 records.

**Product-design judgment:** replace the global English-term toggle with concept-specific display behavior; prefer contextual English, search aliases, and glossary support; use persistent dual labels sparingly.

**Unresolved uncertainty:** all records lack qualified human native review; 40 records have insufficient evidence; spoken usage, private professional practice, and some regional markets remain under-covered. Those limitations are not converted into guessed terminology.

## Quality-control results

- All 22 locale codes and all 12 concept IDs are present.
- All 264 locale–concept pairs are unique and have one table row and one JSONL record.
- Allowed classification, confidence, alternative-status, audience, and translation-status enums were mechanically checked.
- `preferred_term_normalized` was checked for Unicode NFC and lowercase normalization.
- Every non-`INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE` recommendation has retained evidence; `NO_ESTABLISHED_TERM` records without direct lexical attestation contain explicitly marked cumulative negative/paraphrase evidence.
- `HIGH` confidence requires at least two direct publisher-level evidence chains, at least one original source, and at least one strong example; weaker initial ratings were downgraded.
- Exact duplicate evidence URLs were removed per record. Publisher-level chain keys are retained for audit.
- Source flags identify undated, translated, unclear, indexed-only, or otherwise limited evidence.
- Direct source URLs and access dates are stored in both the report and structured artifacts.

Machine-readable companion files contain the full JSONL records, source corpus, manifest, and validation results.
